后端刚重启完，Ralph Loop（自动催促循环）已彻底关闭，不会再自动 BOOT / RESET。所有后续动作由你和我之间的对话驱动。

请按你当前进度选择 A 或 B：

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
情况 A：上一次"全文数据真实性与可追溯性彻底审计"还没完成
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

继续这项审查。回顾要求：

  Q1（真实性）：每个数字（图/表/正文/附录）能在 results.yaml / wandb / 日志 / registry 里找到出处吗？
  Q2（正确性）：抄到论文里没抄错？小数位精度对吗？单位对吗？
  Q3（可追溯性）：同一指标在 Abstract / Intro / Method / Main Table / Analysis / Conclusion 各处完全一致吗？

执行步骤：列清单 → 逐个对账 → 不一致清单 → 决策修/删 → 二次扫描。

完成后必须汇报 4 项：

  (a) 总共审计了多少个数字？
  (b) 多少处不一致 / 找不到出处？分别在哪些章节？
  (c) 怎么处理的（修了 / 删了）？
  (d) 还有哪些数字是"暂时找不到出处但保留"的？为什么？

汇报完后再进入情况 B。

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
情况 B：数据审计已完成
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

进入 GPU 资源回收与数据归档工作。

你这个项目当前分配的 GPU 资源：
  - 服务器: westb-48145    账号: (default)    GPU: (default)

请按以下 4 步执行，每完成一步先汇报再做下一步：

────────────────────────
第 1 步：确认 GPU 是否仍需使用
────────────────────────

仔细评估：
  - 主结果实验都跑完了吗？
  - 是否还需要补实验（ablation / 重跑 / 调参）？
  - 是否预留容量给 reviewer rebuttal？

回复二选一：

  i)  "需要继续使用 [服务器名]" —— 说明原因，后续步骤跳过这台
  ii) "全部可以释放" —— 进入第 2 步

────────────────────────
第 2 步：数据集中
────────────────────────
本项目只有 1 台服务器，跳过集中步骤。直接在这台服务器上把项目数据整理成：

   ~/entrainment_gated_ft_archive/
     ├── code/         （含 .git 历史）
     ├── checkpoints/
     ├── results/      （含 results.yaml / 实验日志 / wandb 本地）
     ├── data/         （仅自定义/非公开数据集）
     └── paper/        （latex 源、figures、pdf）

注意：
- 不要漏 wandb 本地目录、关键 checkpoint（哪怕暂时没用）
- 论文涉及的所有 figures 源数据（.npy / .csv / 绘图脚本）必须打包
- .git 必须保留（包含 commit history）

────────────────────────
第 3 步：汇报集中完成
────────────────────────

集中完成后汇报：

  (a) 集中服务器: [服务器名]
  (b) 归档路径: [完整目录路径]
  (c) 归档总大小: [du -sh 输出]
  (d) 可以释放的服务器列表: [...]

⚠️ 等用户确认后才会去关 GPU / 释放实例。不要自行删除任何数据。

────────────────────────
第 4 步：S3 备份
────────────────────────

把集中后的项目数据打包上传到 S3：

  Bucket:     agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp
  S3 路径:    s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/project-archive/entrainment_gated_ft/
  推荐文件名: entrainment_gated_ft-final-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.tar.gz

AWS 凭证已在 BE 的 ~/.agent-ml-be-backup.yaml 文件里。命令模板（在集中服务器上执行）：

  cd ~ && \
    tar czf /tmp/entrainment_gated_ft-final-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).tar.gz entrainment_gated_ft_archive/ && \
    aws s3 cp /tmp/entrainment_gated_ft-final-*.tar.gz s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/project-archive/entrainment_gated_ft/

如果从远程 GPU 服务器上跑，需要先把凭证 scp 过去 + export：

  export AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID=[REDACTED]
  export AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=[REDACTED]
  export AWS_DEFAULT_REGION=ap-northeast-1

完成后汇报：

  (a) 备份 tarball 最终大小
  (b) S3 完整 URI（带 timestamp 的）
  (c) aws s3 ls s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/project-archive/entrainment_gated_ft/ 的输出（确认对象真的上去了）

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

请告诉我你现在是情况 A 还是 B，然后我们逐步推进。

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 1 步：收集所有非公开数据
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

要备份的内容（在 BE 上或远程 GPU 服务器上）：
  ✅ 代码（含 .git 历史）
  ✅ 训练 / 评测脚本
  ✅ 训练得到的 checkpoints（你自己训出来的，不是 HF / Meta 公开权重）
  ✅ results.yaml / 实验日志 / wandb 本地缓存
  ✅ 自定义 / 私有数据集（你自己构造、爬取、标注的）
  ✅ 论文相关产物（latex 源、figures、PDF）
  ✅ 论文用到的中间产物（图表生成脚本、统计 CSV）

❌ 不要备份：
  - 公开数据集（HuggingFace datasets、GLUE、SuperGLUE 等 anyone 都能 re-download 的）
  - 公开预训练模型（LLaMA、Qwen、GPT-2、BERT 等）
  - Python venv、pip cache、conda env、apt cache、__pycache__
  - 显然临时的 swp / log rotate 备份

你这个项目的 GPU 服务器：
  - westb-48145

收集策略：
本项目只有 1 台 GPU 服务器 (westb-48145)。从这台服务器收集所有数据打包 → 直接上传。

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 2 步：打包成 tarball
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

把第 1 步收集到的目录全部打成 .tar.gz：

  TS=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)
  cd ~ && tar czf /tmp/entrainment_gated_ft-final-$TS.tar.gz entrainment_gated_ft_archive/

确认 tarball 大小合理（一般几百 MB ~ 几十 GB）：

  ls -lh /tmp/entrainment_gated_ft-final-*.tar.gz

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 3 步：上传到 S3（boto3，已验证可行）
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

S3 位置（你这个项目专属）：

  s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/manager-backup/backends/entrainment_gated_ft/project-archive/

凭证：BE 上的 `~/.agent-ml-be-backup.yaml` 已经配好，可以直接读。

在 BE 上跑这个上传脚本（aws CLI 没装，用 boto3）：

```python
# 保存为 /tmp/upload_to_s3.py，然后执行：python3 /tmp/upload_to_s3.py /tmp/entrainment_gated_ft-final-*.tar.gz
import yaml, boto3, sys, time
from pathlib import Path

cfg = yaml.safe_load(open('/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-be-backup.yaml'))
s3 = boto3.client('s3',
    aws_access_key_id=[REDACTED]'aws_access_key_id'],
    aws_secret_access_key=[REDACTED]'aws_secret_access_key'],
    region_name=cfg['s3_region'])

src = sys.argv[1]
size = Path(src).stat().st_size
key = f'manager-backup/backends/entrainment_gated_ft/project-archive/' + Path(src).name
print(f'Uploading {src} ({size/1024/1024:.1f} MB) → s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/{key}')
t0 = time.time()
s3.upload_file(src, 'agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp', key)
elapsed = time.time() - t0
print(f'✅ UPLOAD OK: {elapsed:.1f}s ({size/1024/1024/elapsed:.1f} MB/s)')
print(f'S3 URI: s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/{key}')
print(f'File size: {size} bytes')
```

执行：

  python3 /tmp/upload_to_s3.py /tmp/entrainment_gated_ft-final-*.tar.gz

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 4 步：汇报
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

上传成功（脚本打印 ✅ UPLOAD OK）后，向飞书群汇报：

  (a) tarball 完整路径 + 大小
  (b) S3 完整 URI（脚本输出的）
  (c) 上传速率
  (d) 备份内容清单（你包了哪些目录 / 哪些 server 的数据）

汇报完成 = 任务结束。

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
重要说明
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

⚠️ S3 凭证权限是 **PUT-only**（只能上传，不能 LIST / DELETE）。所以：

  - 你不需要、也无法在 S3 上验证文件是否存在
  - boto3 upload_file 没抛异常 = 上传成功（HTTP 200 + ETag 校验通过）
  - 不要尝试 list / 删除，会 403

⚠️ 不要再向用户确认任何步骤。审计完成 → 立刻收集 → 立刻打包 → 立刻上传 → 立刻汇报。**全程一气呵成。**

⚠️ 如果脚本失败：
  - 报 `AccessDenied` → 检查 S3 路径前缀是不是上面给的 `manager-backup/backends/entrainment_gated_ft/project-archive/`（必须是这个前缀，BE 凭证只允许写这里）
  - 报 `NoSuchKey` / 网络错误 → 重试一次
  - 还失败就把完整错误堆栈发到群里

现在开始。

执行完不要问我，直接备份。

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 0 步：数据审计完了吗？
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

没做完就先做完，做完后**自己往下走，不要等我**。

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 1 步：收集所有非公开数据
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

要打包的：
  ✅ 代码（含 .git 历史）
  ✅ 训练 / 评测脚本
  ✅ 你自己训出来的 checkpoints
  ✅ results.yaml / 实验日志 / wandb 本地缓存
  ✅ 自定义/私有数据集
  ✅ 论文 latex 源、figures、PDF
  ✅ 图表生成脚本、统计 CSV

不要打包：
  ❌ 公开数据集（HF datasets、GLUE 等）
  ❌ 公开模型权重（LLaMA、Qwen 等）
  ❌ venv / pip cache / conda env / __pycache__

你这个项目的 GPU 服务器：
  - westb-48145

收集策略：
本项目有 1 台 GPU 服务器 (westb-48145)。

a) 在 BE 上 SSH 到该服务器，把所有非公开数据 tar 起来：

   ssh westb-48145 "cd ~ && tar czf /tmp/entrainment_gated_ft_remote.tar.gz <你的项目目录>"

b) 把 tarball scp 回 BE：

   scp westb-48145:/tmp/entrainment_gated_ft_remote.tar.gz /tmp/

c) 在 BE 上把 BE 端的数据 + 远程数据合并打包，或者分两次上传都行。


━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 2 步：上传到 S3（**一行命令**）
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

在 BE 上执行：

  python3 /home/ubuntu/upload_to_s3.py /tmp/entrainment_gated_ft-final.tar.gz

（替换 tarball 路径为你实际的文件名。）

脚本会自动：
  - 读 /home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-be-backup.yaml 里的 AWS 凭证
  - 读 /home/ubuntu/.backup_project 里的项目名（已经写好是 `entrainment_gated_ft`）
  - 上传到 s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/manager-backup/backends/entrainment_gated_ft/project-archive/&lt;filename>
  - multipart 自动开启，大文件没问题
  - 打印 ✅ UPLOAD SUCCESS + S3 URI + 速率

⚠️ **这个脚本已经在每个 BE 上做过 smoke test 通过**，凭证和路径全部验证过能用。如果你跑出来失败，多半是你的 tarball 路径写错了 / Python 没装 boto3。

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

汇报完成 = 任务结束。

_source line 761 · 2026-05-25T21:10:42.165288+00:00_

[飞书用户 用户]: 🔒 论文已封版：发起最后一次审稿（只取分数，不改论文）

请立刻发起一次投稿审稿审批（Review 审批）。

⚠️⚠️⚠️ **关键约束** ⚠️⚠️⚠️

**论文已封版**。这次审稿**仅用于获取一个分数**，不用于指导任何修改。

无论审稿报告里说了什么 —— 哪怕指出 typo、说图不清楚、说叙事可以改进、说有 weakness —— **一律不动论文**。我们只要分数。

──────────
具体动作
──────────

1. 走正常的 Review 审批流程，把当前版本的论文提交给 Reviewer。

2. 审稿结束后，Reviewer 会给出一份审稿报告 + 评分。

3. 你需要做的只是：
   (a) 确认收到了审稿报告
   (b) 把审稿报告里的最终 **分数 / Overall Score** 摘出来汇报给我
   (c) **不要** 根据审稿意见修改论文
   (d) **不要** 生成 revision plan
   (e) **不要** 调用 paper-agent 改任何 .tex 文件

──────────
为什么要这么做
──────────

这是论文最终封版前的最后一道质量检查 —— 拿一个内部审稿的 baseline 分数，作为我们对最终版本质量的参考记录。

封版之后再改论文会引入新的不一致（数字、引用、图表都已经审计对账过），所以宁可分数差一点也不动它。

──────────
汇报格式
──────────

审稿完成后回复：

  ✅ 审稿完成
  Overall Score: X / Y
  各维度分数: [...]
  Reviewer 数量: N
  审稿报告路径: [path]
  论文版本: 已封版未改动 ✓

记住：**只汇报分数，不要改论文。** 这是硬要求。

_source line 779 · 2026-05-25T21:38:01.713836+00:00_

[审稿报告]
Overall: 3.0 Findings


# 审稿报告 — Round 22（Fresh Review）

**论文**: Aggregate Equivalence ≠ Behavioral Equivalence: Per-Example Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Models
**目标 Venue**: EMNLP 2026 (via ARR)
**Reviewer 编排**: 2 人（R1: 方法严谨性 + 实验设计；R2: 新颖性 + 相关工作完整性 + 格式合规）
**审稿模式**: Fresh（无上轮记忆）

---

## Summary

本文研究 hybrid linear recurrent models（Mamba、Mamba-2、GDN 等）中 aggregate-level 等价掩盖 per-example 行为分歧的现象。利用 Wang et al. (2025) 受控 model zoo 在 4 个 benchmark 上进行系统性 per-example 分析，发现：(1) 不同架构在整体准确率相近时，per-example 正确/错误集合存在显著结构化差异；(2) per-example overlap 在 attention ratio 约 4% 处趋于稳定；(3) confidence margin 可以中等精度（AUC 0.70-0.74）预测受影响样本。附带 confidence-based routing case study。

---

## Strengths

1. **受控实验设计精良**：Wang et al. (2025) model zoo 实现了 training recipe 严格对齐的单变量控制（仅改变架构 backbone），这在 multiplicity 文献中罕见。
2. **统计方法透明诚实**：permutation tests + Bonferroni 校正 + normalized effect sizes，每一处统计不足都有坦诚 caveat（Section 3.5）。
3. **附录深度**：14-22 页附录提供全面 robustness checks（GDN exclusion、continuous-threshold Jaccard、5-architecture validation 等），极大增强可信度。
4. **Reproducibility**：inference-only 实验（~5 GPU-hours），代码和数据匿名公开。
5. **5-architecture validation**：40 个 permutation tests 全部显著（p ≤ .002），per-example divergence 的核心发现在跨架构层面稳健。
6. **研究问题重要且及时**：hybrid recurrent models 正在被广泛部署，揭示 aggregate metrics 掩盖的 per-example 差异具有实际价值。
7. **格式完全合规**：8 页主文、匿名化、Limitations 位置正确、Abstract ≤ 200 words。

---

## Weaknesses

### Critical（影响接收决定）

**W1** [R1-C1 + R2-W2, 共识 Critical] **Spearman n=5 统计功效不足，核心发现呈现与 caveat 矛盾**
- n=5 attention ratios 的 Spearman 相关统计功效近乎为零。LAMBADA 校正后 p=.167 不显著，但仍作为核心发现呈现。Bonferroni α/10=.005 在 n=5 下理论上不可达（最小 p=.017）。
- Section 3.5 标注 "suggestive" 的 caveat 与 Section 4.2 标题和 Conclusion 中更确定性的表述之间存在张力（尽管 R21 已改为 "appears to stabilize"，但根本问题不变）。
- **跨 22 轮持续 Critical**（对应 R5-W2 + R16-N1）。

**W2** [R1-C2 + R2-W1, 共识 Critical→Structural] **单 seed 训练无法分离架构效应与随机种子效应**
- 每个架构仅有 1 次训练运行，观察到的 per-example 差异可能部分或完全由 seed variation 驱动。
- Pythia 70M 9-seed 校准存在三重 mismatch（scale/family/accuracy），无法可靠推广到 hybrid 1.3B 模型。
- **结构性限制**，无法在现有数据上修复。R22 确认为 wont_fix 但必须在 Limitations 中充分讨论。

**W3** [R2-N2 + R1 交叉 PARTIALLY AGREE] **核心贡献偏描述性，方法论增量有限**
- 三个 contributions 可归结为：在新模型族上复现已知 predictive multiplicity + 观察到 Jaccard 趋平（n=5 无法统计确认）+ moderate AUC predictor。方法论均为已有工具（Jaccard, α, permutation tests）。
- R1 指出 empirical context novelty（within-family controlled comparison）有别于 McCoy et al. (2020) 的 across-family 比较，足以支撑 Findings 但不够 Conference。
- **共识：Major。Findings 标准下可接受。**

### Major（影响评分但不一票否决）

**W4** [R2-N1 + R1 交叉 AGREE→Major] **Discussion and Conclusion 过于单薄**
- Section 5 仅约 120 词。以 "deployment reliability" 为卖点的论文未讨论：从业者模型选择流程变化、benchmark 设计含义、fine-tuning 后 multiplicity 持续性、与 ecological fallacy 的理论联系。
- R1 认为是 presentation 问题而非 soundness 问题，降为 Major。

**W5** [R2-N3 + R1 交叉 AGREE→Major] **相关工作缺失重要引用**
- 缺失 4 篇高度相关工作：
  - Swayamdipta et al. (2020) Dataset Cartography — per-example analysis 方法论前驱，confidence/variability 分解与本文 helped/hurt 概念高度类似
  - Black et al. (2022) Model Multiplicity survey — multiplicity 综述
  - Fehlauer et al. (2025) Convergence and Divergence under Different Random Seeds — 直接关联 seed confound
  - Ganesh et al. (2025) Systematizing Multiplicity — multiplicity 最新框架
- R1 确认全部 4 篇高度相关，但认为 revision 可修复，降为 Major。

**W6** [R1-C3] **n=3 架构下 cross-architecture 统计推断基础薄弱**
- 仅 3 个架构族（Transformer、Mamba/Mamba-2、GDN），bootstrap CI 在 n=3 下覆盖率远低于名义值。Section 3.5 已标 "heuristic" 但 Table 2 呈现方式暗示更强可靠性。

**W7** [R1-M1] **GDN h3-1 的 16-head confound**
- GDN 模型使用 14 个注意力头（vs Transformer/Hawk 12 heads），构成混淆变量。排除 GDN 后 LAMBADA ρ=-0.7 (p=.233) 不显著，MMLU ρ 反转。Per-example 核心发现不受影响，但 convergence 叙事高度敏感。

**W8** [R1-M3 + R2-W3] **Routing case study 证据单薄，定位过高**
- LAMBADA +5.3pp，但 PIQA -0.6pp (p=.006) 显著为负。仅 2/4 benchmark 正向效果。
- 缺少 baseline（majority-vote ensemble、average-logits ensemble、oracle routing 上界）。
- Abstract/Conclusion 仍暗示更广泛实用性。

**W9** [R1-M5] **缺少 fine-tuning 实验**
- 论文以 deployment 为卖点但全部实验基于 pre-trained model。Fine-tuning 后 per-example multiplicity 是否持续完全未验证。

**W10** [R1-M2] **Binarization 信息损失**
- 将连续预测概率二值化为 correct/incorrect 损失信息。Appendix O continuous-threshold Jaccard 部分缓解，但正文未充分讨论。

**W11** [R1-M4] **AUC 0.70-0.74 实用性未讨论**
- Moderate AUC 在实际部署中是否足够有用需要讨论。"Reliable" 用词应修正为 "moderate"。

### Minor

**W12** [R2 小问题] Table 3 列标签 J̄_u 中 "u" 代表 "hurt" 不直观，建议改为 J̄_hurt。
**W13** [R2 小问题] Task taxonomy 为 informal/non-pre-registered，后续 cross-category 比较说服力受限。
**W14** [R2 小问题] LaTeX aggressive spacing hacks（\textfloatsep=4pt 等），虽不违规但影响阅读体验。

---

## Reviewer Consensus

| 问题 | R1 | R2 | 交叉结果 |
|------|----|----|----------|
| Spearman n=5 功效不足 | C1 (Critical) | W2 (Should-Fix) | R1 [AGREE] R2 → 共识 Critical |
| 单 seed confound | C2 (Critical) | W1 (Should-Fix) | R1 [AGREE] R2 → 共识 Critical/Structural |
| Contributions 增量有限 | — | N2 (Critical) | R1 [PARTIALLY AGREE] → 共识 Major |
| Discussion 单薄 | — | N1 (Critical) | R1 [AGREE] 但降 Major |
| 缺失关键引用 | — | N3 (Critical) | R1 [AGREE] 但降 Major |
| n=3 统计推断薄弱 | C3 (Critical) | — | R1 独有 |
| Routing 价值有限 | M3 (Major) | W3 (Should-Fix) | R1 [AGREE] R2 → 共识 Major |
| GDN head confound | M1 (Major) | — | R1 独有 |
| Fine-tuning 缺失 | M5 (Major) | 含在 N1 | 共识方向一致 |
| Overall Score | 3.0 Findings | 3.0 Findings | **3.0 Findings** |
| Confidence | 4/5 | 4/5 | — |

---

## Reviewer Divergence

**严重程度分级分歧**：R1 将统计功效（n=5 Spearman）和 seed confound 列为 Critical，R2 列为 Should-Fix。反之，R2 将 Discussion 单薄和 novelty 不足列为 Critical，R1 降为 Major。但双方在总体评价（3.0 Findings）上完全一致，分歧仅在于哪些问题更致命，而非论文整体水平。

---

## Questions（需要作者澄清）

1. Pythia 70M 校准显示 within-seed J 与 cross-architecture J 存在 2.7-4.3× gap。这个 gap 是否在更大 scale（如 410M+）上仍然成立？410M 校准失败的原因是什么？
2. 排除 GDN 后 LAMBADA ρ=-0.7 (p=.233) 不显著，MMLU ρ 反转——这对 Contribution 2（saturation claim）意味着什么？
3. Confidence-based routing 在 PIQA 上显著为负 (-0.6pp, p=.006)——作者如何解释这一结果？是否暗示 routing 策略需要 task-specific 调整？
4. 论文提到 "left to future work" 的 average-logits ensemble baseline——为什么不在本文中提供这一最自然的对比？
5. Per-example multiplicity 在 fine-tuning 后是否持续？作者有任何初步证据或理论预期吗？

---

## Missing References

以下高度相关工作未被引用（均已在 bib 中但未在正文中引用，或完全缺失）：

1. **Swayamdipta et al. (2020)** "Dataset Cartography: Mapping and Diagnosing Datasets with Training Dynamics" — per-example analysis 方法论前驱，confidence/variability 分类与本文概念高度类似。**必须引用。**
2. **Black et al. (2022)** "Model Multiplicity: Opportunities, Concerns, and Solutions" — multiplicity 综述。论文以 multiplicity 为核心概念却未引用该综述。
3. **Fehlauer et al. (2025)** "Convergence and Divergence of Language Models under Different Random Seeds" — 直接关联 seed confound 问题，可提供 seed variation 基线。
4. **Ganesh et al. (2025)** "Systemizing Multiplicity" — multiplicity 最新系统化框架。
5. **Geifman & El-Yaniv (2017/2019)** selective prediction — routing 策略的方法论前驱。
6. **Caruana et al. (2004)** ensemble selection — routing/selection 的经典框架。

## Overall Assessment

**Overall Score: 3.0 — Findings**
**Confidence: 4/5**
**Soundness: 3.5/5** | **Excitement: 2.5/5** | **Reproducibility: 4/5**

本文是一篇实验设计严谨、统计分析透明的经验性研究。受控 model zoo 的单变量设计和 5-architecture validation 是实质性优点。然而，核心贡献偏描述性（在新模型族上复现已知 multiplicity 现象），方法论无新增工具。两个关键统计问题（n=5 Spearman 功效不足、单 seed 无法分离架构/种子效应）构成结构性限制，无法通过修订完全解决。Discussion 过于单薄，routing case study 证据不足但定位过高。

**适合 Findings track**：核心 per-example divergence 发现稳健（40 个 permutation tests 全部显著），对 hybrid model 部署有参考价值，但 evidence strength 和 intellectual depth 不足以支撑主会议。

---

## Detailed Comments

### Section 1 (Introduction)
- Contributions 列表三点均为描述性发现，应强化 "first controlled within-family comparison" 的独特实验设计价值。
- 当前版本是否已将 "saturates" 改为 "appears to stabilize" 需确认。

### Section 2 (Related Work)
- 缺失 4 篇高度相关引用（见 Missing References）。
- Dataset Cartography 的 confidence/variability 分解与本文 helped/hurt 概念的对比讨论完全缺失。
- Selective prediction 和 ensemble selection 文献未引用，削弱 routing 贡献的 positioning。

### Section 3 (Methodology)
- §3.5 的 "heuristic" caveat 与后续章节确定性表述之间的张力是核心 presentation 问题。
- Permutation test 的 "conservative" 论证需要修正——不条件化 item difficulty 可能使检验 anti-conservative。

### Section 4 (Results)
- §4.2 saturation claim：hedging 已改善（"appears to stabilize"），但 n=5 根本问题不变。
- GDN exclusion 后多个 benchmark 的 ρ 不显著或反转，需在正文中更显著位置讨论。
- Routing：需要 ensemble baseline 和更诚实的 Abstract/Conclusion 定位。

### Section 5 (Discussion & Conclusion)
- 从 ~120 词扩展至至少 0.5 页。
- 应涵盖：ecological fallacy 理论联系、从业者实践建议、fine-tuning 后 multiplicity 假设、benchmark 设计含义。

### Limitations
- 当前 3 点覆盖合理。
- 建议补充 greedy decoding 对生成任务的潜在影响（一句即可）。

### Format
- 全部合规：8 页、匿名化、正确模板、Limitations 位置正确。
- 色盲可辨识性问题（Figure 3, 5b）应在 camera-ready 前修正。

### 引用真实性
- 3 个 2026 arXiv 引用已验证真实。
- 2 个引用（Basu 2026, Haghighat et al. 2026 AAAI）未独立验证。

## ⚠️ 处理协议(强制,逐条执行,禁止简略)

**收到上面的审稿报告后必须按以下顺序处理**:

1. **逐条编号通读**: 把报告里所有问题、修改建议一项一项列出来(R1, R2, R3...),
   即使有的看起来重复或次要也要单独保留 — 不允许漏任何一条。

2. **每条给出详细修复方案**:
   - 补实验: 写明实验配置(baseline / 超参 / seed / dataset / metric) + 预估 GPU 时长
   - 改论文: 写明改哪一节、改写思路、新引用是否需要补
   - 改 framing / 措辞: 写明原文 + 改写后版本
   - 写 rebuttal: 写明论点和支撑证据
   - 拒绝: 写明拒绝理由(reviewer 误读 / 超出 scope / 已有反驳证据)

3. **必须调 `post_to_reviewer` 让 Research Reviewer 补充意见**:
   把完整审稿报告 + 你的逐条初步方案打包发给 Reviewer, 让 ta 独立审视有没有
   遗漏、方案可行性、技术细节是否合理。**这一步不可省** — Reviewer 是你的
   独立审查员, 他对每条审稿意见的判断和你互补。

4. **收到 Reviewer 补充后综合定稿**: 把 reviewer 反馈合并进你的 Action Matrix,
   再安排 Main Agent 执行(补实验 / 改论文)或向用户确认 venue 变更。
   **对 CRITICAL/WARNING 级的修复指令, 必须要求 Agent 修改完成后把修后段落 +
   原始审稿意见发给 Reviewer 做验证 — Reviewer 以审稿人视角判断修复是否充分,
   返回 PASS 才算完成。** 不验证就标 resolved 是"修了但没修对"的根源。

5. **同步 Issue Tracker**: Action Matrix 定稿后, 用 `read_local_file` 读
   `projects/{project}/artifacts/review_issues.yaml`, 把本轮处理的 issue 标
   `in_progress`、Reviewer 已确认修复的标 `resolved`, 调 `update_review_issues`
   写回。这是跨轮次的修复追踪 — Continue 模式审稿要靠这个表识别复发问题。

6. **区分写作问题和 evidence 问题**: 对每条 CRITICAL/WARNING 级审稿意见,
   先判断: "如果论文写得完美无缺(措辞零瑕疵),这个问题还存在吗?"
   - 是 → 这是 evidence 问题, 不能只改论文。必须补实验/补分析/降级 claim
   - 否 → 这是写作问题, 可以通过改论文修复
   审稿人说"not convincing / insufficient evidence / lacks support / confound /
   reliability / sample size"时, 基本都是 evidence 问题 — 不要用加 hedging 应付。

7. **检查复发问题**: 在制定修复方案之前, 先 `read_local_file` 读
   `projects/{project}/artifacts/review_issues.yaml`, 找出本轮审稿意见中
   哪些是之前已经出现过的老问题。对复发问题重点关注:
   - 查看上轮用了什么方法修复(action_items 中记录了)
   - 如果上轮已经用写作手段修复过但审稿人仍然 flag → 写作手段对这个问题无效,
     必须换路径(补实验 / 补分析 / 降级 claim 位置)
   - 在 Action Matrix 中标注 "[复发 × N 轮]", 并说明为什么这次的修复方案
     和之前不同

8. **claim 校准是双向的**: 实验结果超预期时 claim 可以升级(扩大 scope / 去掉
   hedging / 提升到 headline), 实验结果不及预期时 claim 应该降级。降级 claim
   不是降级论文 — 把一个 evidence 不足的 finding 从 headline 移到 discussion,
   反而让论文整体更可信。

9. **向 A 群汇报**: Action Matrix 定稿后,用 `director_reply_to_user` 向用户汇报:
   - 审稿整体评估(系统性问题有哪些、严重程度)
   - Action Matrix 摘要(补哪些实验、改哪些章节、写哪些 rebuttal)
   - 预估时间线
   - 需要用户决策的事项(如有)

**禁止**:
- 跳过第 3 步直接派 Main Agent
- 把多条审稿意见合并成一条 action
- 对低 priority 意见用"暂不处理"一笔带过而不给出明确理由
- 整体性"已知悉"回复
- 对 evidence 问题只用写作手段修复(第 6 条)
- 对复发问题重复使用已失败的修复方法(第 7 条)

[飞书用户 用户]: 📋 论文必查项：Ethics Statement 位置（强调：不是放在 Appendix）

ARR 官方政策对论文末尾两个特殊章节的规定：

──────────
位置：main paper 里，Conclusion 之后、References 之前
──────────

  ... Conclusion ...
  ## Limitations              ← 必须有，没有会 desk reject
  ## Ethical Considerations   ← 强烈推荐（如果工作有伦理相关考量）
  ## References
  ## Appendix                 ← Ethics Statement 不在这里！

⚠️ Ethics Statement 不能放在 Appendix。必须在 main paper 末尾、References 之前。

──────────
特点：
──────────

- 这两节都不算页数（写多少都不挤占 8 页正文）
- Limitations: required
- Ethical Considerations: 推荐但不强制（标题官方建议就用 "Ethical considerations"）

──────────
什么样的工作应该写 Ethical Considerations？
──────────

任何涉及以下情况都建议写：
- 数据集来源 / 偏见 / 隐私
- 模型潜在滥用风险（jailbreak、误导生成、伤害性输出）
- 人类标注劳工（众包平台、报酬、IRB）
- 对特定群体（人种、性别、地域）可能的不公平影响

──────────
请立刻检查 + 修正：
──────────

(a) 你的论文有没有 Limitations？放对位置了吗（Conclusion 之后 References 之前）？
(b) 有没有 Ethical Considerations？如果在 Appendix 里，**立刻挪到 References 之前**。
(c) 如果完全没有 Ethical Considerations，根据你的工作判断是否要加（涉及上述任何一项就加）。

⚠️ 论文已封版：允许的修改是「补加 Limitations / Ethical Considerations 章节」或「把它们从 Appendix 挪到 References 之前」。其他章节正文内容不要动。

完成后汇报：Limitations 段在第几页？Ethical Considerations 段在第几页？两节字数各多少？

34	\begin{document}
35	\maketitle
36	
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\leq 0.14$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\

{
  "text": "【PART 0】\n\n**Title:** Aggregate Equivalence ≠ Behavioral Equivalence: Per-Example Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Models\n\n**Abstract:**\n\"\"\"\nHybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo (Wang et al., 2025) (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity (Marx et al., 2020): pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard <= 0.14, roughly twice the independence baseline; all p < .01 across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format (alpha = 0.14-0.30) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, suggesting that minimal attention may establish which examples each architecture affects. Yang et al. (2026) document analogous per-item disagreement (16-66%) among frontier large language models (LLMs) despite comparable aggregate scores; our within-family design isolates backbone variation from training-regime differences. Confidence margins partially predict affected examples (area under the ROC curve [AUC] 0.70-0.74). Low cross-architecture overlap implies substantial complementarity, though routing-based exploitation remains task-dependent.\n\"\"\"\n\n**PDF path:** `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/main.pdf`\n- ✅ Latest frozen version (includes Limitations + AI Disclosure)\n- ✅ Compiles without LaTeX error\n- ✅ Main body ≤ 8 pages\n\n---\n\n【PART 1】\n\n**Research Area:** Interpretability and Analysis of Models for NLP\n\n**Keywords:** behavioral analysis, model comparison, predictive multiplicity, per-example analysis, hybrid architectures\n\n**Contribution Types:**\n- ☑ Model analysis & interpretability\n- ☑ NLP engineering experiment\n- ☑ Data analysis\n\n**Languages:** English\n\n---\n\n【PART 2】\n\n2.1 Reassignment Request Area Chair: confirmed\n2.2 Reassignment Request Reviewers: confirmed\n2.3 Preprint: confirmed\n2.4 Preprint Status: confirmed\n2.5 Preferred Venue: confirmed\n2.6 Visa Needs: confirmed\n2.7 Country Of Origin: confirmed\n2.8 Consent To Share Data: confirmed\n2.9 Consent To Share Submission Details: confirmed\n2.10 License Agreement: confirmed\n2.11 EMNLP 2026 AI Reviewing Experiment: confirmed\n2.12 Author Submission Checklist: confirmed\n\n---\n\n【PART 3】\n\n**A1:** Yes — Limitations section appears after Conclusion, before References (unnumbered §Limitations in main paper).\n\n**A2:** No — This work analyzes publicly available pretrained models on standard benchmarks using inference-only evaluation. The findings characterize existing model behavior rather than introducing new capabilities, posing no direct risks.\n\n**B:** Yes\n\n**B1:** Yes — All artifacts are cited: Wang et al. (2025) for the model zoo, benchmark creators (Paperno et al. 2016, Clark et al. 2018, Zellers et al. 2019, etc.), and Gao et al. (2024) for lm-evaluation-harness. See §3.\n\n**B2:** No — Artifact licenses are not explicitly discussed. All models and benchmarks used are publicly released for research purposes under their respective open licenses.\n\n**B3:** No — Consistency with intended use is not explicitly discussed. All artifacts are used for their standard research purposes: models for inference evaluation, benchmarks for accuracy measurement.\n\n**B4:** N/A — No personally identifiable information or offensive content is involved. All benchmarks are standard NLP evaluation datasets.\n\n**B5:** Yes — Benchmark details (sample sizes, task types, scoring methods) are documented in §3.2 and Appendix A. Model zoo summary in Table 1.\n\n**B6:** Yes — Sample sizes reported for all benchmarks (§3.4, Table 3). Per-example counts in Appendix H. Statistical test parameters (10,000 permutations/bootstrap resamples, seed 42) specified in §3.5.\n\n**C:** Yes\n\n**C1:** Yes — Model sizes (340M, 1.3B) reported in §3.1 Table 1. Total compute ~5 GPU-hours on a single NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000, detailed in Appendix Y.\n\n**C2:** Yes — All evaluations use lm-evaluation-harness v0.4.x with default prompts and greedy decoding (§3.2). No hyperparameter search was conducted; this is an inference-only analysis of pretrained checkpoints.\n\n**C3:** Yes — Bootstrap 95% CIs, permutation test z-scores, and Bonferroni corrections reported throughout §3.5 and §4. All results are single-run, acknowledged in Limitations.\n\n**C4:** Yes — lm-evaluation-harness v0.4.x with default settings specified in §3.2. Bootstrap and permutation parameters (10,000 resamples, seed 42) in §3.5.\n\n**D:** No\n\n**D1:** N/A — No human subjects or annotators were used.\n\n**D2:** N/A — No human subjects or annotators were used.\n\n**D3:** N/A — No human subjects or annotators were used.\n\n**D4:** N/A — No human subjects or annotators were used.\n\n**E:** Yes\n\n**E1:** Yes — Appendix Z \"Use of AI Assistance\": Claude was used for coding, code drafting, debugging, and language polishing."

I found the paper successfully! Here's what I found:

"Predictive Multiplicity in Classification" is a paper by Marx, C., Calmon, F., and Ustun, B., published in the Proceedings of the 37th International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2020).

**Overview:**
The paper defines predictive multiplicity as the ability of a prediction problem to admit competing models with conflicting predictions. The authors introduce measures to evaluate the severity of predictive multiplicity and develop integer programming tools to compute these measures exactly for linear classification problems.

Based on the search results, I found important information about this paper. Note that there's a discrepancy with your query regarding the year:

"Launch and Iterate: Reducing Prediction Churn" by Milani Fard and colleagues was published at the Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 29 conference in December 2016 (not 2019).

The paper formulates the problem of prediction churn and presents a stabilization operator to regularize a classifier towards a previous classifier, using a Markov chain Monte Carlo stabilization operator to produce a model with more consistent predictions without adversely affecting accuracy.

The paper addresses practical applications of machine learning that involve successive training iterations with changes to features and training examples, where ideally changes should only be improvements, but in practic

_source line 876 · 2026-05-26T06:39:17.667392+00:00_

[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 匿名化最后扫雷：日期 + 内部编号

发现有些论文里残留了**具体实验日期**和**内部编号**——这两种东西是 reviewer 一查就能反向定位作者 / 团队的硬证据，必须彻底清掉。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
1. 必须删 / 改：具体日期
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

任何能锁到一周内的时间点都不允许出现。包括但不限于：

❌ "Experiments were conducted on May 18, 2026..."
❌ "We collected data between 2026-04-10 and 2026-04-25..."
❌ "Models were trained on 2026/05/15..."
❌ "Snapshot from 2026-Q1..."
❌ "as of October 2025"
❌ "the recent release of GPT-5 on July 22..."

替换为相对 / 模糊表述：

✅ "Experiments were conducted in recent months."
✅ "Data was collected over a two-week period."
✅ "Models were trained for X GPU-hours."

唯一例外：引用别人论文 / 数据集发布时间（"Llama-3 (Meta, 2024)" 这种是 citation，不算）。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
2. 必须删 / 改：内部编号
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

任何内部追溯编号都不允许出现：

❌ Experiment ID: "exp_2026_0518_a3" / "run_42_v7"
❌ WandB run ID / wandb.ai 链接
❌ 项目内部 codename（如 cost-verify-agent-rl 这种）
❌ Git commit hash
❌ Internal ticket / Jira ID
❌ Server hostname / GPU node 名（"trained on cqa1-33015"）
❌ 内部文件夹路径（"/home/USER/exp_logs/..."）
❌ Orchestration 工具名（Ralph / Director / Manager / Agent 等）

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
3. 容易漏检的位置
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

不只检查正文。下列位置最容易残留：

- Figure / Table caption
- Appendix 里的 log snippet / yaml dump / shell output
- Hyperparameter 表
- \texttt{} / \verb{} 代码块
- .tex 注释（% 后面的内容）
- .bib 文件里相关字段
- PDF 元数据

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
汇报内容
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

(a) 各类命中位置数量：日期 X 处，内部编号 Y 处
(b) 每一处的处理方式（改成什么 / 删了什么）
(c) 修完后确认 0 残留

30	\title{Aggregate Equivalence $\neq$ Behavioral Equivalence: Per-Example Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Models}
31	
32	\author{Anonymous}
33	
34	\begin{document}
35	\maketitle
36	
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\leq 0.14$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (\S\ref{ssec:model_zoo}), we evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks plus supplementary COPA ($n = 100$) at 1.3B scale unless noted. Results at 340M appear in scale analyses. Recall: h$k$-1 denotes one attention layer per $k{+}1$ layers; h3-1 = 25\% attention.
4	
5	\subsection{Per-Example Divergence}\label{ssec:per_example}
6	
7	Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}). Binarization discards confidence magnitude, so we supplement with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}) and verify that low Jaccard persists under continuous thresholds (Appendix~\ref{app:continuous_jaccard}).
8	
9	\begin{figure}[t]
10	\centering
11	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jaccard_hierarchy.pdf}
12	\caption{Jaccard hierarchy on LAMBADA (1.3B). Cross-architecture overlap ($\bar{J} \approx 0.09$) is $2\times$ the independence baseline but only one-third of within-architecture overlap ($\bar{J} \approx 0.33$). Pythia 70M within-seed Jaccard (different scale) falls at a comparable level. Error bars show the helped--hurt range.}
13	\label{fig:jaccard_hierarchy}
14	\end{figure}
15	
16	\begin{figure*}[t]
17	\centering
18	\includegraphics[width=0.75\textwidth]{figures/fig3_alpha_routing.pdf}
19	\caption{(a) Per-example ambiguity $\alpha$ varies $2\times$ across task types. (b) Confidence-based routing gains track $\alpha$: effective when multiplicity is high (LAMBADA) but counterproductive when low (PIQA). 1.3B, h3-1.}
20	\label{fig:alpha_routing}

_source line 906 · 2026-05-26T06:39:42.543860+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	When aggregate benchmarks show equivalent performance, the choice among hybrid architectures is often driven by efficiency considerations. Per-example multiplicity reveals a complementary dimension: each backbone handles a different subset of inputs correctly, determined by the recurrent mechanism rather than the attention configuration (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}). For deployment contexts where per-example reliability matters, aggregate equivalence provides incomplete assurance. The complementarity structure (Table~\ref{tab:complementarity}) shows that oracle-level combination yields $+7$--$14$\,pp gains, but majority vote captures only ${\sim}51$\% because majority-exploitable and majority-hostile disagreements occur in near-equal proportion, motivating architecture-aware routing beyond simple ensembling. Our within-family design isolates backbone variation as the source of this multiplicity; \citet{yang2026benchmark} document analogous per-item disagreement (16--66\%) among frontier LLMs under conflated training regimes, suggesting the phenomenon is partly architectural in origin and resists homogenization even at minimal attention ratios.
4

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures \citep[GatedDeltaNet, RetNet, HGRN2, GLA, DeltaNet;][]{yang2024gateddeltanet,sun2023retnet,qin2024hgrn2,yang2024gla,yang2024deltanet} achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets, yet their helped and hurt example sets overlap by only ${\sim}10$\% (pairwise Jaccard $\approx 0.10$). This gap between aggregate equivalence and per-example behavioral equivalence is invisible in standard metrics: architectures that score similarly reach that score by gaining and losing on different inputs, and the choice of backbone carries hidden consequences for which specific examples a deployed system handles correctly.
4

16	\caption{Per-example overlap appears stable from minimal attention across benchmarks. (c) This pattern holds across all four main benchmarks: mean pairwise $\bar{J}$ remains flat across attention ratios (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq .333$). Shaded bands are illustrative ($n = 3$).}
17	\label{fig:decoupling}
18	\end{figure*}
19

1	\section{Method}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	We describe the model configurations, evaluation benchmarks, and analytical framework used to measure how attention affects cross-architecture agreement at both aggregate and per-example levels.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\small
8	\caption{Model zoo summary \citep{wang2025hybrid}. All configurations share training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters; they differ only in backbone and attention ratio.}
9	\label{tab:model_zoo}
10	\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
11	\toprule
12	Architecture & Recurrence mechanism & Scales \\
13	\midrule
14	GatedDeltaNet & Gated delta rules & 340M, 1.3B \\
15	RetNet & Multi-scale exp.\ decay & 340M, 1.3B \\
16	HGRN2 & Gated state expansion & 340M, 1.3B \\
17	\midrule
18	\multicolumn{3}{@{}l@{}}{\footnotesize Attention ratios: 0\%, 4\%, 7.7\%, 14.3\%, 25\%} \\
19	\multicolumn{3}{@{}l@{}}{\footnotesize Extended (340M only): +GLA, +DeltaNet} \\
20	\bottomrule
21	\end{tabular}
22	\end{table}

24	\subsection{Model Zoo}\label{ssec:model_zoo}
25	
26	We use the pretrained model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (Table~\ref{tab:model_zoo}), which provides controlled comparisons: GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet}, RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet}, and HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} at five attention ratios and two scales. The GDN 1.3B h3-1 configuration uses 16 attention heads vs.\ 32 for RetNet and HGRN2 (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}). We adopt the h$k$-1 notation: h3-1 = 25\% attention, h6-1 = 14.3\%, h12-1 = 7.7\%, h24-1 = 4\%. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters, isolating architecture and attention configuration. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline at both scales.
27	
28	\subsection{Task Taxonomy}\label{ssec:task_taxonomy}

1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. Recent theoretical work establishes a provable expressiveness hierarchy between hybrid and pure linear attention \citep{ye2026provable}, providing formal grounding for the architectural differences we characterize at the per-example level. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules, extending GLA \citep{yang2024gla} and DeltaNet \citep{yang2024deltanet}. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, and connect to linear attention \citep{katharopoulos2020transformers} through the state-space--attention duality of Mamba-2. xLSTM \citep{beck2024xlstm} revives the LSTM lineage with 
…[truncated 1780 chars]

30	\title{Aggregate Equivalence $\neq$ Behavioral Equivalence: Per-Example Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Models}
31	
32	\author{Anonymous}
33	
34	\begin{document}
35	\maketitle
36	
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\leq 0.14$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\

1	% Appendix sections for the paper.
2	
3	\section{Confirmatory and Exploratory Analyses}\label{app:benchmark_details}
4	
5	The task taxonomy used in \S\ref{ssec:task_taxonomy} was defined prior to running any convergence analyses. Each benchmark was assigned to exactly one of four categories based on the primary cognitive demand of the task:
6	
7	\begin{itemize}
8	    \item \textbf{Retrieval}: tasks requiring prediction from long-range context without multi-step reasoning (LAMBADA).
9	    \item \textbf{Comprehension}: reading comprehension with explicit passage support (BoolQ).
10	    \item \textbf{Reasoning}: tasks requiring commonsense inference or multi-step reasoning (HellaSwag, ARC-Challenge, WinoGrande, PIQA).
11	    \item \textbf{Mixed}: tasks combining retrieval, comprehension, and reasoning demands (ARC-Easy, MMLU, SciQ, XStoryCloze).
12	\end{itemize}
13	
14	\paragraph{Confirmatory benchmark set.} Six benchmarks were designated as confirmatory for Bonferroni correction: LAMBADA, HellaSwag, ARC-Challenge, WinoGrande, ARC-Easy, and MMLU. These were selected as representative of retrieval, reasoning, and mixed categories before any convergence analysis was performed. The taxonomy and benchmark selection were fixed before analysis as an informal pre-analysis plan; we describe this as a confirmatory/exploratory split rather than a formal pre-registration, since no external timestamp exists. The main text applies the more conservative all-10-benchmark correction (Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .017 \times 10 = .167$). \textbf{Sensitivity:} correcting across only the six confirmatory benchmarks yields adjusted $p = .017 \times 6 = .100$, still above conventional significance.
15

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures \citep[GatedDeltaNet, RetNet, HGRN2, GLA, DeltaNet;][]{yang2024gateddeltanet,sun2023retnet,qin2024hgrn2,yang2024gla,yang2024deltanet} achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets, yet their helped and hurt example sets overlap by only ${\sim}10$\% (pairwise Jaccard $\approx 0.10$). This gap between aggregate equivalence and per-example behavioral equivalence is invisible in standard metrics: architectures that score similarly reach that score by gaining and losing on different inputs, and the choice of backbone carries hidden consequences for which specific examples a deployed system handles correctly.

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (\S\ref{ssec:model_zoo}), we evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks plus supplementary COPA ($n = 100$) at 1.3B scale unless noted. Results at 340M appear in scale analyses. Recall: h$k$-1 denotes one attention layer per $k{+}1$ layers; h3-1 = 25\% attention.
4	
5	\subsection{Per-Example Divergence}\label{ssec:per_example}
6	
7	Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}). Binarization discards confidence magnitude, so we supplement with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}) and verify that low Jaccard persists under continuous thresholds (Appendix~\ref{app:continuous_jaccard}).

Pythia 70M within-seed Jaccard (different scale) falls at a comparable level. Error bars show the helped--hurt range.}

1	\section*{Responsible NLP Research Checklist}
2	
3	\subsection*{A. For every submission:}
4	
5	\begin{itemize}
6	\item[\checkmark] \textbf{A1.} Did you describe the limitations of your work?
7	
8	\textit{Yes. The Limitations section (after \S5) discusses statistical power constraints ($n = 5$ attention ratios), per-example scope (four benchmarks, two supplementary), scale coverage (two scale points), architecture coverage (five architectures from one model zoo---three primary at two scales, two additional at 340M---single training run per configuration), observational design (descriptive, not causal), and pre-training-only evaluation.}
9	
10	\item[\checkmark] \textbf{A2.} Did you discuss any potential risks of your work?
11	
12	\textit{Yes. The Ethics Statement notes that this work analyzes existing public models and introduces no new risks. Per-example behavioral differences may inform practitioners about limitations of aggregate benchmarks.}
13	
14	\item[\checkmark] \textbf{A3.} Do all claims in the paper match the paper's contributions and scope?
15	
16	\textit{Yes. Claims are scoped to five architectures (three primary, two extended at 340M) and four benchmarks spanning retrieval, reasoning, and mixed tasks. We explicitly note that Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$ does not reach conventional significance, that the scale comparison is a preliminary observation from two data points, and that per-example multiplicity is characterized as a task-dependent variation ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than claimed as universal. BoolQ and WinoGrande are reported as supplementary in the Appendix due to structural limitations of near-chance binary/two-choice classification on Jaccard significance.}
17	
18	\item[\checkmark] \textbf{A4.} Have you used AI writing assistants when working on this paper?
19	
20	\textit{Yes. AI writing assistants were used for drafting and editing. All scientific content, claims, and analyses were verified by the authors.}
21	\end{itemize}

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/main.tex:38:Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\leq 0.14$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, suggesting that minimal attention may establish which examples each architecture affects. \citet{yang2026benchmark} document analogous per-item disagreement (16--66\%) among frontier large language models (LLMs) despite comparable aggregate scores; our within-family design isolates backbone variation from training-regime differences. Confidence margins partially predict affected examples (area under the ROC curve [AUC] 0.70--0.74). Low cross-architecture overlap implies substantial complementarity, though routing-based exploitation remains task-dependent\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/entrainment_gated_ft-9444/}.}.

34	\item Bootstrap 95\% CIs on $\alpha$ added (Appendix Table~6 + \S4.2): all adjacent-benchmark CIs non-overlapping except PIQA--HellaSwag.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/sections/appendix.tex:All models are from the publicly available model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}. Analysis code and per-example evaluation outputs are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/entrainment_gated_ft-9444/}.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/main.tex:Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\leq 0.14$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, suggesting that minimal attention may establish which examples each architecture affects. \citet{yang2026benchmark} document analogous per-item disagreement (16--66\%) among frontier large language models (LLMs) despite comparable aggregate scores; our within-family design isolates backbone variation from training-regime differences. Confidence margins partially predict affected examples (area under the ROC curve [AUC] 0.70--0.74). Low cross-architecture overlap implies substantial complementarity, though routing-based exploitation remains task-dependent\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open

I have searched all .tex and .bib files in `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_2/projects/entrainment_gated_ft/docs/paper/` for dates that could identify when work was done. Here are the results:

- **Main files:** main.tex, author_response.tex, author_response_r7.tex, arr_checklist.tex

- **Bibliography:** references.bib

### Dates Found:

**IMPORTANT NOTE:** All dates found with year 2025 or 2026 are **BIBLIOGRAPHIC ENTRIES** in the references.bib file and represent citation years, NOT work dates. These should NOT be changed as they are legitimate academic citations.

**Bibliography citations with years 2025-2026:**
- Line 16: `@inproceedings{zimerman2025unified, year={2025}`
- Line 85: `@article{basu2026routing, year={2026}`

**NO ACTUAL WORK DATES FOUND:**
- No comment lines (%) containing explicit dates like "May 18, 2026" or "2026-05-15"
- No month+year combinations outside of citations (e.g., "May 2026", "March 2025")
- No quarter references like "Q1 2026"
- No TODO/FIXME/HACK comments with dates
- No date patterns in YYYY-MM-DD format

### Conclusion:

The paper contains no embedded work dates that could reveal when actual research was conducted. All 2025/2026 references are properly part of the bibliographic system and represent publication years of cited papers, which are appropriate and should be retained as-is. There are no accidental date references that could leak timing information about this project's work.

30	\title{Aggregate Equivalence $\neq$ Behavioral Equivalence: Per-Example Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Models}
31	
32	\author{Anonymous}
33	
34	\begin{document}
35	\maketitle
36	
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\leq 0.14$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\

778	
779	All pairwise Jaccard values remain $\leq 0.14$, consistent with the three-architecture results at 1.3B (Table~\ref{tab:per_example}). The seven new pairs involving GLA and DeltaNet show no systematic difference from the original three pairs (Mann-Whitney $p > .3$ for both helped and hurt), indicating that the low-overlap pattern is not specific to the original architecture triple. A sixth architecture (HGRN; \citealp{qin2023hierarchically}) is also available in the zoo but excluded from the main analysis: its substantially lower baseline accuracy on LAMBADA (0.263 vs.\ 0.32--0.38 for the other five) mechanically inflates helped-set sizes and Jaccard values. All 15 six-architecture permutation tests nonetheless remain significant ($z \geq 2.9$, $p \leq .006$). The same pattern holds on HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$): all 20 additional pairwise tests yield $p < .01$ with mean $J_{\text{helped}} \leq 0.10$. Full four-benchmark and six-architecture data are included in our code release.
780	
781	\section{Code, Data, and Computational Resources}
782	\label{app:code_data}
783	
784	All models are from the publicly available model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid}. Analysis code and per-example evaluation outputs are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/entrainment_gated_ft-9444/}.
785	
786	\paragraph{Compute.} All experiments are inference-only (no training). Evaluations were conducted on a single NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPU with approximately 5 GPU-hours total across all 30 model configurations and 10 benchmarks. Statistical analyses (permutation tests, bootstrap CIs, Jaccard computations) ran on CPU in under 1 hour.

788	\section{Use of AI Assistance}

46	\end{table}
47	
48	\paragraph{Multiplicity variation.} Extending to HellaSwag ($N = 10{,}042$) and PIQA ($N = 1{,}838$) reveals that multiplicity varies systematically across tasks (Table~\ref{tab:multiplicity}). Ambiguity $\alpha$ \citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) through 0.15 (HellaSwag) and 0.21 (ARC-Easy; Appendix~\ref{app:arceasy}) to 0.30 (LAMBADA). Format does not drive this ordering: HellaSwag (four-way) and PIQA (two-choice) produce nearly identical $\alpha$ despite different answer-space sizes ($\rho = -0.60$, $p = .24$ with task accuracy). This suggests multiplicity is associated with cognitive demands rather than evaluation format, though the confound cannot be fully resolved (Limitations). Bootstrap 95\% CIs confirm the ordering (all non-overlapping except PIQA--HellaSwag; Table~\ref{tab:arceasy_ambiguity}). Pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\bar{J} \leq 0.10$, all $p < .01$), indicating architectures affect different examples regardless of how many are affected. Within each benchmark, $\alpha$ is stable across attention ratios (variation $< 0.03$) but differs $2\times$ across task types, confirming that multiplicity magnitude depends on task, not attention ratio \citep[cf.][]{gorbett2026crossmodel}.
49

_source line 998 · 2026-05-26T06:41:32.919180+00:00_

1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}\label{sec:discussion}
2	
3	When aggregate benchmarks show equivalent performance, the choice among hybrid architectures is often driven by efficiency considerations. Per-example multiplicity reveals a complementary dimension: each backbone handles a different subset of inputs correctly, determined by the recurrent mechanism rather than the attention configuration (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}). For deployment contexts where per-example reliability matters, aggregate equivalence provides incomplete assurance. The complementarity structure (Table~\ref{tab:complementarity}) shows that oracle-level combination yields $+7$--$14$\,pp gains, but majority vote captures only ${\sim}51$\% because majority-exploitable and majority-hostile disagreements occur in near-equal proportion, motivating architecture-aware routing beyond simple ensembling. Our within-family design isolates backbone variation as the source of this multiplicity; \citet{yang2026benchmark} document analogous per-item disagreement (16--66\%) among frontier LLMs under conflated training regimes, suggesting the phenomenon is partly architectural in origin and resists homogenization even at minimal attention ratios.
4

_source line 1016 · 2026-05-26T08:18:31.211958+00:00_

[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 论文自检：前向引用问题（先用后定义）

发现有些论文里出现"先用后定义"的情况——某个概念 / 符号 / 模块 / 缩写在 Section X 里直接用了，但它的定义放在 Section X+N 才出现。这是 AI 写作的典型问题，reviewer 读到这里会觉得"不知所云、组织混乱"。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
检查范围
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

每个东西第一次出现的位置，必须**已经**完成定义。包括：

□ 缩写 / 简写（如 RLHF / DPO / IRT，第一次出现要写全称 + 缩写）
□ 自定义概念 / 术语（你 paper 自己造的词，如 "behavioral death"、"sycophancy score"）
□ 数学符号 / 公式中的变量（每个变量在第一次出现的方程里就要解释）
□ 自定义方法 / 模块组件（"the consistency gate"、"the alignment head"）
□ 评测指标（自定义的 metric，必须在 main result 表出现之前定义清楚）
□ 数据集 / setting 的内部命名（"the strict split"、"the holdout pool"）

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
常见违规模式
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

❌ Abstract / Intro 里用了一个自造术语，Method 才定义
❌ Intro 提到 "we use SYCONScore"，到 §4 才看到这个 metric 的定义
❌ Method §2 用了变量 α，到 §2.3 才写 "where α denotes..."
❌ Method 提到 "module M"，结构图 + 解释在 Appendix 才有
❌ Table 1 列了 "Score@K"，K 在 §5 才说取什么值

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
修复策略
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

每个违规位置二选一：

A) **把定义提前**到第一次使用之前。最干净。
B) 在第一次使用的位置**加一个简短的 inline 解释**（一句话 / 括号说明）+ "(formally defined in §X)" 引用前向。容忍度更高的妥协方案。

注意：A 优先。只有 A 会改动太多结构时，才退而求其次用 B。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
汇报内容
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

(a) 找到多少处先用后定义？分布在哪些章节？
(b) 每一处用 A 还是 B 修的？
(c) 修完后 PDF 编译通过

_source line 1018 · 2026-05-26T08:18:46.770271+00:00_

37	\begin{abstract}
38	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures that appear interchangeable by aggregate benchmarks can differ on which specific examples they handle correctly. Using a controlled model zoo \citep{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects of adding attention and find structured predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive}: pairwise overlap of helped and hurt example sets is uniformly low (Jaccard $\leq 0.14$, roughly twice the independence baseline; all $p < .01$ across five architectures (three primary, two validation)). This multiplicity varies by task type or evaluation format ($\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention ratio. Per-example overlap appears to stabilize at 4\% attention and remains flat across higher ratios, suggesting that minimal attention may establish which examples each architecture affects. \citet{yang2026benchmark} document analogous per-item disagreement (16--66\%) among frontier large language models (LLMs) despite comparable aggregate scores; our within-family design isolates backbone variation from training-regime differences. Confidence margins partially predict affected examples (area under the ROC curve [AUC] 0.70--0.74). Low cross-architecture overlap implies substantial complementarity, though routing-based exploitation remains task-dependent\footnote{Code and data are available at \url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/entrainment_gated_ft-9444/}.}.
39	\end{abstract}

1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Hybrid linear recurrent architectures \citep[GatedDeltaNet, RetNet, HGRN2, GLA, DeltaNet;][]{yang2024gateddeltanet,sun2023retnet,qin2024hgrn2,yang2024gla,yang2024deltanet} achieve aggregate benchmark scores within 1--2 points of each other at matched parameter budgets, yet their helped and hurt example sets overlap by only ${\sim}10$\% (pairwise Jaccard $\approx 0.10$). This gap between aggregate equivalence and per-example behavioral equivalence is invisible in standard metrics: architectures that score similarly reach that score by gaining and losing on different inputs, and the choice of backbone carries hidden consequences for which specific examples a deployed system handles correctly.

(c) This pattern holds across all four main benchmarks: mean pairwise $\bar{J}$ remains flat across attention ratios (all $\rho \leq 0.80$, $p \geq .333$). Shaded bands are illustrative ($n = 3$).}

1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Hybrid Recurrent Architectures.}
4	The design space for combining attention with recurrence spans layer-level interleaving \citep[Jamba;][]{lieber2024jamba}, local attention pairing \citep[Griffin, RecurrentGemma;][]{de2024griffin,botev2024recurrentgemma}, matrix-valued recurrence \citep[RWKV-6;][]{peng2024eagle}, within-head mixing \citep[Hymba;][]{dong2024hymba}, and unbounded-context hybrid SSMs \citep[Samba;][]{ren2024samba}. Mamba-2 \citep{dao2024mamba2} establishes a duality between structured state spaces and attention, while \citet{ali2025hidden} reveal implicit attention within Mamba. Comparative evaluations confirm that hybrids outperform pure Transformers and SSMs at scale \citep{waleffe2024empirical}, and that even one attention layer enables content-based routing \citep{basu2026routing}. Recent theoretical work establishes a provable expressiveness hierarchy between hybrid and pure linear attention \citep{ye2026provable}, providing formal grounding for the architectural differences we characterize at the per-example level. \citet{wang2025hybrid} provide the controlled model zoo we use, holding training conditions constant while varying architecture and attention ratio.
5	
6	\paragraph{Linear Recurrent Models.}
7	Our three recurrent backbones represent distinct gating strategies within the linear recurrence family: RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet} uses multi-scale exponential decay, HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} applies gated state expansion, and GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet} combines gated updates with delta rules, extending GLA \citep{yang2024gla} and DeltaNet \citep{yang2024deltanet}. These descend from S4 \citep{gu2022s4} and Mamba \citep{gu2024mamba}, which introduced structured state spaces and input-dependent selection, and connect to linear attention \citep{katharopoulos2020transformers} through the state-space--attention duality of Mamba-2. xLSTM \citep{beck2024xlstm} revives the LSTM lineage with 
…[truncated 1780 chars]

1	\section{Method}\label{sec:method}
2	
3	We describe the model configurations, evaluation benchmarks, and analytical framework used to measure how attention affects cross-architecture agreement at both aggregate and per-example levels.
4	
5	\begin{table}[t]
6	\centering
7	\small
8	\caption{Model zoo summary \citep{wang2025hybrid}. All configurations share training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters; they differ only in backbone and attention ratio.}
9	\label{tab:model_zoo}
10	\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}
11	\toprule
12	Architecture & Recurrence mechanism & Scales \\
13	\midrule
14	GatedDeltaNet & Gated delta rules & 340M, 1.3B \\
15	RetNet & Multi-scale exp.\ decay & 340M, 1.3B \\
16	HGRN2 & Gated state expansion & 340M, 1.3B \\
17	\midrule
18	\multicolumn{3}{@{}l@{}}{\footnotesize Attention ratios: 0\%, 4\%, 7.7\%, 14.3\%, 25\%} \\
19	\multicolumn{3}{@{}l@{}}{\footnotesize Extended (340M only): +GLA, +DeltaNet} \\
20	\bottomrule
21	\end{tabular}
22	\end{table}
23	
24	\subsection{Model Zoo}\label{ssec:model_zoo}
25	
26	We use the pretrained model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (Table~\ref{tab:model_zoo}), which provides controlled comparisons: GatedDeltaNet \citep{yang2024gateddeltanet}, RetNet \citep{sun2023retnet}, and HGRN2 \citep{qin2024hgrn2} at five attention ratios and two scales. The GDN 1.3B h3-1 configuration uses 16 attention heads vs.\ 32 for RetNet and HGRN2 (\S\ref{ssec:robustness}). We adopt the h$k$-1 notation: h3-1 = 25\% attention, h6-1 = 14.3\%, h12-1 = 7.7\%, h24-1 = 4\%. Within each scale, all models share identical training data, tokenizer, and hyperparameters, isolating architecture and attention configuration. The zoo also includes a Transformer baseline at both scales.
27	
28	\subsection{Task Taxonomy}\label{ssec:task_taxonomy}
29

_source line 1026 · 2026-05-26T08:18:52.185294+00:00_

1	\section{Experiments}\label{sec:experiments}
2	
3	Using the model zoo of \citet{wang2025hybrid} (\S\ref{ssec:model_zoo}), we evaluate all 30 configurations on 10 benchmarks plus supplementary COPA ($n = 100$) at 1.3B scale unless noted. Results at 340M appear in scale analyses. Recall: h$k$-1 denotes one attention layer per $k{+}1$ layers; h3-1 = 25\% attention.
4	
5	\subsection{Per-Example Divergence}\label{ssec:per_example}
6	
7	Do architectures converge by improving on the same examples? We compare each architecture's pure-recurrent baseline against its h3-1 hybrid on 5{,}153 LAMBADA examples, classifying each as helped, hurt, or unchanged. Although the decomposition is binary, it captures genuine separation in the continuous confidence space (Cohen's $d = 0.73$--$0.92$ between helped and hurt on LAMBADA; cross-benchmark details in Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}). Binarization discards confidence magnitude, so we supplement with continuous confidence margin analysis (Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}) and verify that low Jaccard persists under continuous thresholds (Appendix~\ref{app:continuous_jaccard}).
8	
9	\begin{figure}[t]
10	\centering
11	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jaccard_hierarchy.pdf}
12	\caption{Jaccard hierarchy on LAMBADA (1.3B). Cross-architecture overlap ($\bar{J} \approx 0.09$) is $2\times$ the independence baseline but only one-third of within-architecture overlap ($\bar{J} \approx 0.33$). Pythia 70M within-seed Jaccard (different scale) falls at a comparable level. Error bars show the helped--hurt range.}
13	\label{fig:jaccard_hierarchy}
14	\end{figure}
15	
16	\begin{figure*}[t]

542	
543	\paragraph{Normality verification.} Shapiro-Wilk tests reject normality for all six architecture--set combinations (all $W < 0.98$, $p < 10^{-6}$), but the practical departure varies by group. Disruption-set $|\log p|$ values show mild skewness (0.53--0.77, $W \in [0.955, 0.973]$), closely approximating a normal distribution at the sample sizes involved. Rescue-set values are moderately right-skewed (skewness 1.33--1.41, $W \in [0.899, 0.920]$), following an approximately log-normal distribution. Because the skew is one-sided, parametric AUC estimates (0.70--0.74 in the main text) are conservative lower bounds; non-parametric empirical AUC from the same data yields 0.73--0.77.
544	
545	Despite similar within-architecture AUC, the optimal prediction thresholds differ across architectures for disruption but not rescue (Table~\ref{tab:threshold_gap}). Rescue thresholds cluster tightly (range 0.03 in $|\log p|$), indicating that the rescuability boundary (the confidence level below which pure-wrong examples are likely to be corrected by attention) is architecture-invariant. Disruption thresholds span a 47\% relative range (GDN: 0.70, RetNet: 1.00, HGRN2: 1.14), meaning that the same confidence level on a pure-correct example may predict vulnerability in one architecture but safety in another. This asymmetry constitutes a second dimension of multiplicity beyond per-example disagreement: all three architectures agree on which wrong answers are rescuable, but disagree on which correct answers are vulnerable to disruption.

51	\section*{Limitations}
52	
53	Each configuration is a single training run; consistency across all 40 architecture--benchmark combinations mitigates but cannot fully resolve seed confounds.
54	We examine three architectures (plus two extended) from one model zoo at two scales; generalization to other hybrid families (Mamba, RWKV), larger scales (7B+), and open-ended generation remains open.
55	All evaluations use pretrained checkpoints with greedy decoding; per-example effects may not persist after fine-tuning or under alternative sampling strategies.

### Tool result

_source line 1040 · 2026-05-26T08:22:06.250664+00:00_

25	
26	Our central finding is that architectural variation produces structured predictive multiplicity whose magnitude depends on task type, not attention ratio. The ambiguity $\alpha$ \citep{marx2020predictive} ranges from 0.14 (PIQA) to 0.30 (LAMBADA), varies $2\times$ across task types but only 0.03 within each benchmark across attention ratios; pairwise Jaccard remains uniformly low ($\leq 0.14$, all $p < .01$ across all ten five-architecture pairs). A second finding is that per-example overlap appears to stabilize at the first attention level (4\%) and remains flat at higher ratios. Even on LAMBADA, where aggregate variance shows a non-significant decreasing trend ($\rho = -1.0$, Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .167$; at 1.3B only), per-example Jaccard does not change (\S\ref{ssec:decoupling}).
27

_source line 1044 · 2026-05-26T08:22:15.543053+00:00_

25	
26	\paragraph{Confidence margin predictor.} Pure-model confidence on the target token predicts which examples will be affected (AUC 0.70--0.74, a non-parametric measure equivalent to Mann-Whitney $U$; Appendix~\ref{app:confidence_margin}): rescue thresholds are architecture-invariant (1\% spread) but disruption thresholds vary 47\%, explaining cross-architecture Jaccard divergence (Appendix~\ref{app:prediction_framework}). Calibration differences account for less than half the threshold spread (Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}). The rescue pattern generalizes across three benchmarks ($d = 0.13$--$0.60$; Appendix~\ref{app:cm_cross}); disruption is weaker on reasoning tasks (Appendix~\ref{app:boolq}). This structured disagreement constitutes predictive multiplicity \citep{marx2020predictive} under architectural variation.
27

### User

_source line 1052 · 2026-05-26T10:13:47.728533+00:00_

[飞书用户 曹晓玉]: 🔍 论文自检：所有引用的逐项正确性检查（交叉验证）

请对论文里**每一条引用**做严格核对。AI 写作最致命的问题之一就是 hallucinate 引用——作者错、年份错、venue 错、标题对不上，甚至整篇论文压根不存在。

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
检查范围
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

对 .bib 里的每一条 entry，以及正文里每一个 \cite{} 出现的位置：

□ 作者名拼写（中间名 / 缩写格式 / Unicode 字符）
□ 年份（很多 AI 把 NeurIPS 2022 paper 写成 2021）
□ 标题（前几个词最容易被 hallucinate 改写）
□ Venue / 会议名（NeurIPS vs NIPS / EMNLP vs EMNLP Findings / arXiv vs published version）
□ 卷期号 / 页码（journal 引用）
□ DOI / arXiv ID（如果有）
□ Paper 是否真实存在（hallucinated paper 是最严重的硬伤）
□ 正文里的 cite 用得对吗（cite 了 paper A 但说的是 paper B 的结论）

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
执行方式：Main Agent 派 2-3 个 Worker 做交叉验证
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

Main Agent 调用 spawn_worker 起 2-3 个独立 Worker，每个 Worker 独立完成全部引用的核对：

- 每个 Worker 拿到完整的 .bib 文件 + 正文 \cite 位置列表
- 用 Semantic Scholar API / Google Scholar / arXiv 搜索每一条
- 独立产出一份"引用核对清单"：每条标 ✓ 或 ✗ + 错误描述

Main Agent 收到所有 Worker 的清单后做交叉比对：

- ✅ 所有 Worker 都标 OK 的 → 接受
- ⚠️ Worker 之间结论不一致的 → Main Agent 亲自再核一遍
- ❌ 多数 Worker 标错的 → 必须修

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
修复策略
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

- 作者 / 年份 / 标题错 → 改成正确的
- venue 错（如 arxiv 版本和会议版本搞混）→ 改成应该引用的版本
- Paper 不存在（hallucinated）→ 删 cite + 重新找一个真实的同主题 paper 替代，如果找不到替代且这个 claim 离开 cite 就站不住，整段重写或删
- cite 用错位置（A 引到了 B）→ 改成正确的 cite key

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
汇报内容
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

(a) 总共多少条引用？
(b) 每个 Worker 各标出多少处问题？Worker 之间一致率？
(c) Main Agent 交叉比对后，最终确认有问题的引用数量
(d) 每一处的处理方式（修了 / 删了 / 替换了哪条新引用）
(e) 修完后 .bib 重新生成 + LaTeX 编译通过 + bibtex 没 warning

I found the paper you were looking for! Here are the details:

**Paper:** "Cross-Model Disagreement as a Label-Free Correctness Signal" by Matt Gorbett and Suman Jana (arXiv:2603.25450, submitted March 26, 2026)

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of detecting when a language model is wrong without ground truth labels, which is fundamental for safe deployment. Existing approaches rely on a model's own uncertainty (such as token entropy or confidence scores), but these signals fail critically on confident errors, where a model is wrong but certain.

The authors introduce cross-model disagreement as a correctness indicator—a simple, training-free signal that can be dropped into existing production systems, routing pipelines, and deployment monitoring infrastructure without modification. Given a model's generated answer, cross-model disagreement computes how surprised or uncertain a second verifier model is when reading that answer via a single forward pass. No g
…[truncated 188 chars]

Links: [{"title":"A Provable Expressiveness Hierarchy in Hybrid Linear-Full Attention","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2602.01763v1"},{"title":"A Provable Expressiveness Hierarchy in Hybrid Linear-Full Attention","url":"https://www.arxiv.org/pdf/2602.01763"},{"title":"[2602.01763] A Provable Expressiveness Hierarchy in Hybrid Linear-Full Attention","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.01763"},{"title":"A Systematic Analysis of Hybrid Linear Attention","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2507.06457"},{"title":"WuNeng: Hybrid State with Attention","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2504.19191"}]

I found the paper for you! The paper is titled "A Provable Expressiveness Hierarchy in Hybrid Linear-Full Attention" by Xiaowei Ye and 4 other authors (Xiaoyu He, Chao Liao, Chen Wu, and Pinyan Lu).

The paper addresses how transformers serve as the foundation of modern large language models, and examines efficient attention mechanisms like linear and hybrid attention developed to mitigate the quadratic complexity of standard full attention, though their expressive power relative to full attention lacks rigorous theoretical characterization.

The work theoretically characterizes performance differences among attention mechanisms, with the theory applying to all linear attention variants that can be formulated as a recurrence (including Mamba and DeltaNet), and establishes an expressiveness hierarchy for sequential function composition tasks.

The paper was submitted on February 2, 2026 and is available on arXiv as preprint 2602.01763.

I found the paper you're looking for. The paper "WinoGrande: An Adversarial Winograd Schema Challenge at Scale" is authored by Keisuke Sakaguchi, Ronan Le Bras, Chandra Bhagavatula, and Yejin Choi from the Allen Institute for Artificial Intelligence and University of Washington.

The paper introduces WinoGrande, a large-scale dataset of 44k problems, inspired by the original Winograd Schema Challenge design, but adjusted to improve both the scale and the hardness of the dataset. The best state-of-the-art methods on WinoGrande achieve 59.4–79.1%, which are approximately 15–35% below human performance of 94.0%.

I found the paper you were looking for. Here's what I can tell you about it:

The paper "Launch and Iterate: Reducing Prediction Churn" was authored by Mahdi Milani Fard, Quentin Cormier, Kevin Canini, and Maya Gupta and was published at Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 29 (NIPS 2016).

The paper addresses the problem of prediction churn that occurs when machine learning models are updated successively with changes to features and training examples, where new predictions may change neutrally for many examples, resulting in unnecessary churn.

## Key Contributions

The paper introduces architectural design advancements that include multi-headed matrix-valued states and a dynamic recurrence mechanism that improve expressivity while maintaining the inference efficiency characteristics of RNNs.

Links: [{"title":"[2602.11898] Benchmark Illusion: Disagreement among LLMs and Its Scientific Consequences","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11898"},{"title":"[2602.11898v1] Benchmark Illusion: Disagreement among LLMs and Its Scientific Consequences","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11898v1"},{"title":"Benchmark Illusion: Disagreement among LLMs and Its Scientific Consequences","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2602.11898"},{"title":"Computation and Language","url":"https://www.arxiv.org/list/cs.CL/pastweek?skip=4&show=500"},{"title":"IllusionBench+: A Large-scale and Comprehensive Benchmark for Visual Illusion Understanding in Vision-Language Models","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.00848"}]

I found the paper you were looking for. Here's what the search revealed:

"Benchmark Illusion: Disagreement among LLMs and Its Scientific Consequences" is authored by Eddie Yang and Dashun Wang, submitted to arXiv on February 12, 2026 with identifier 2602.11898.

The paper demonstrates that apparent convergence in benchmark accuracy can conceal deep epistemic divergence, showing that LLMs achieving comparable accuracy still disagree on 16-66% of items when tested on two major reasoning benchmarks - MMLU-Pro and GPQA - and 16-38% among top-performing frontier models.

When such models are used for scientific data annotation and inference, their hidden disagreements propagate into research results, with switching the annotation model potentially changing estimated treatment effects by more than 80% and in some cases reversing their sign, illustrating a "benchmark illusion" where equal accuracy may conceal disagreement with model choice becoming a hidden yet consequential variable for scientific reproducibility.

The paper is available on arXiv at: https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.11898

I found the paper you're looking for! Here are the details:

"Convergence and Divergence of Language Models under Different Random Seeds" by Fehlauer, Mahowald, and Pimentel was published in the Proceedings of the 2025 Conference on Empirical Methods in Natural Language Processing, pages 32982–32991, in Suzhou, China.

The paper investigates the convergence of language models trained under different random seeds, measuring convergence as the expected per-token Kullback–Leibler (KL) divergence across seeds. By comparing LM convergence as a function of model size and training checkpoint, the authors identify a four-phase convergence pattern: (i) an initial uniform phase, (ii) a sharp-convergence phase, (iii) a sharp-divergence phase, and (iv) a slow-reconvergence phase.

Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile the findings.

Key issues found:

1. **qin2023hierarchically**: Bib lists 8 authors (Qin, Li Songlin, Zhong Weixuan, Sun Yiran, Li Dong, Peng Hao, Yang Zhijun, Zhong Sheng). Actual authors are only 3: Zhen Qin, Songlin Yang, Yiran Zhong. The bib appears to mix up names — "Yiran Zhong" became "Sun Yiran" + "Zhong Sheng", and additional spurious authors were added.

2. **jain2025allocation**: First author listed as "Jain, Suyash" — actual is "Shomik Jain". Second author "Wang, Yuanjun" — actual is "Margaret Wang". Fourth author "Wilson, Christo" — actual is "Ashia Wilson".

3. **bisk2020piqa**: Title in bib is "Reasoning about Physical **Intuition** in Natural Language" — actual title is "Reasoning about Physical **Commonsense** in Natural Language".

4. **sakaguchi2021winogrande**: Year listed as 2021 — actual publication year is **2020** (AAAI-20, The Thirty-Fourth AAAI Conference on Artificial Intelligence).

5. **lin2022xstorycloze**: Author list includes "Arber, Mikel" — should be "Mikel **Artetxe**". Also "Wang, Xian" appears in bib while the actual last author is "Xian Li" (and "Tianlu Wang" is a different person also in the list). The bib also omits many authors before the "others" abbreviation.

All other entries verified as correct.
